Mathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Descent.DescentDataAsCoalgebra

CategoryTheory.Pseudofunctor.DescentDataAsCoalgebrastructureMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Descent.DescentDataAsCoalgebra, type
Wording
descent data as coalgebra of F and f (from the name)
English
Let $F$ be a pseudofunctor of $\operatorname{LocallyDiscrete}$ applied to the set of opposites of $C$ and the set of adjoints of the set of categories. Let $f$ be a function from elements $i$ of $x_{1}$ to homomorphisms of $X$ applied to $i$ and $S$. Then the descent data as coalgebra of $F$ and $i$ are a basic type.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Pseudofunctor} (\operatorname{LocallyDiscrete} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C)), \operatorname{Adj} (\operatorname{Cat})), f \in x_{1} \rightarrow \operatorname{Hom} (X (i), S)\vdash \operatorname{DescentDataAsCoalgebra} (F , i , f)\ \mathsf{set}$
CategoryTheory.Pseudofunctor.DescentDataAsCoalgebra.HomstructureMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Descent.DescentDataAsCoalgebra, type
Wording
homomorphism of x2 and x3 (from the name)
English
Let $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$ be descent data as coalgebra of $F$ and $f$. Then the homomorphisms of $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$ are a basic type.
Formula
$x_{2} , x_{3} \in \operatorname{DescentDataAsCoalgebra} (F , f)\vdash \operatorname{Hom} (x_{2} , x_{3})\ \mathsf{set}$
CategoryTheory.Pseudofunctor.DescentDataAsCoalgebra.Hom.homdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Descent.DescentDataAsCoalgebra, function or constant
Wording
the homomorphism of self and i (from the name)
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a homomorphism of $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$. Let $i$ be an element of $x_{1}$. Then the homomorphism of $\mathrm{self}$ and $i$ is a homomorphism of the object of $x_{2}$ and $i$ and the object of $x_{3}$ and $i$ defined as $\operatorname{proj0}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$ and $i$.
Formula
$\mathrm{self} \in \operatorname{Hom} (x_{2} , x_{3}), i \in x_{1} \vdash \operatorname{hom} (\mathrm{self} , i):= \operatorname{proj0} (\mathrm{self} , i)\in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{obj} (x_{2} , i), \operatorname{obj} (x_{3} , i))$
CategoryTheory.Pseudofunctor.DescentDataAsCoalgebra.coalgebraEquivalencedefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Descent.DescentDataAsCoalgebra, function or constant
Wording
the coalgebra equivalence of F and f (from the name)
English
Let $F$ be a pseudofunctor of $\operatorname{LocallyDiscrete}$ applied to the set of opposites of $C$ and the set of adjoints of the set of categories. Let $x_{1}$ be a set. Let $f$ be a homomorphism of $X$ and $S$. Then the coalgebra equivalence of $F$ and $f$ is an equivalence of the set of descent data as coalgebra of $F$ and the function that maps $x_{3}$ to $f$ and the set of coalgebras of the comonad of the adjunction of the adjoint of the map of $\operatorname{toPrefunctor}$ applied to $\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctorStruct}$ applied to $\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctor}$ applied to $F$ and $\operatorname{toLoc}$ applied to the opposite in $f$.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Pseudofunctor} (\operatorname{LocallyDiscrete} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C)), \operatorname{Adj} (\operatorname{Cat})), x_{1} \in \mathrm{Set} , f \in \operatorname{Hom} (X , S)\vdash \operatorname{coalgebraEquivalence} (F , x_{1} , f)\in \operatorname{Equivalence} (\operatorname{DescentDataAsCoalgebra} (F , \lambda x_{3} . f), \operatorname{Coalgebra} (\operatorname{toComonad} (\operatorname{ofCat} (\operatorname{adj} (\operatorname{map} (\operatorname{toPrefunctor} (\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctorStruct} (\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctor} (F))), \operatorname{toLoc} (\operatorname{op} (f))))))))$

English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a descent data as coalgebra of $F$ and $f$. Let $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$ be elements of $x_{1}$. Then the homomorphism of $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$ is a homomorphism of the object of $\mathrm{self}$ and $x_{2}$ and the action of the functor of $\operatorname{l}$ applied to the map of $\operatorname{toPrefunctor}$ applied to $\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctorStruct}$ applied to $\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctor}$ applied to $F$ and $\operatorname{toLoc}$ applied to the opposite in $f$ applied to $x_{2}$ and the action of the functor of $\operatorname{r}$ applied to the map of $\operatorname{toPrefunctor}$ applied to $\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctorStruct}$ applied to $\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctor}$ applied to $F$ and $\operatorname{toLoc}$ applied to the opposite in $f$ applied to $x_{3}$ and the object of $\mathrm{self}$ and $x_{3}$ defined as $\operatorname{proj1}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$, $x_{2}$ and $x_{3}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{self} \in \operatorname{DescentDataAsCoalgebra} (F , f), x_{2} , x_{3} \in x_{1} \vdash \operatorname{hom} (\mathrm{self} , x_{2} , x_{3}):= \operatorname{proj1} (\mathrm{self} , x_{2} , x_{3})\in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{obj} (\mathrm{self} , x_{2}), \operatorname{obj} (\operatorname{toFunctor} (\operatorname{l} (\operatorname{map} (\operatorname{toPrefunctor} (\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctorStruct} (\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctor} (F))), \operatorname{toLoc} (\operatorname{op} (f (x_{2})))))), \operatorname{obj} (\operatorname{toFunctor} (\operatorname{r} (\operatorname{map} (\operatorname{toPrefunctor} (\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctorStruct} (\operatorname{toPrelaxFunctor} (F))), \operatorname{toLoc} (\operatorname{op} (f (x_{3})))))), \operatorname{obj} (\mathrm{self} , x_{3}))))$

CategoryTheory.Pseudofunctor.DescentData'structureMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Descent.DescentDataPrime, type
Wording
descent data of sq and i (from the name)
English
Let $F$ be a pseudofunctor of $\operatorname{LocallyDiscrete}$ applied to the set of opposites of $C$ and the set of categories. Let $\mathrm{sq}$ be a function from elements $i$ of $x_{1}$ and elements $j$ of $x_{1}$ to chosen pullbacks of $f$ applied to $i$ and $f$ applied to $j$. Let $x_{2}$ be a function from elements $x_{3}$ of $x_{1}$ and elements $x_{4}$ of $x_{1}$ and elements $x_{5}$ of $x_{1}$ to chosen pullbacks of $\mathrm{sq}$ applied to $x_{4}$ and $x_{5}$ and $\mathrm{sq}$ applied to $x_{3}$ and $x_{5}$. Then the descent data of $i$ and $j$ are a basic type.
Formula
$F \in \operatorname{Pseudofunctor} (\operatorname{LocallyDiscrete} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C)), \operatorname{Cat}), \mathrm{sq} \in x_{1} \rightarrow x_{1} \rightarrow \operatorname{ChosenPullback} (f (i), f (j)), x_{2} \in x_{1} \rightarrow x_{1} \rightarrow x_{1} \rightarrow \mathrm{CategoryTheory\_\_Limits\_\_ChosenPullback\_u2083\_} (\mathrm{sq} (x_{3} , x_{4}), \mathrm{sq} (x_{4} , x_{5}), \mathrm{sq} (x_{3} , x_{5}))\vdash \operatorname{DescentData'} (F , i , j , \mathrm{sq} , x_{3} , x_{4} , x_{5} , x_{2})\ \mathsf{set}$

first object of P and R (from the name)
English
Let $P$ be a functor of the set of opposites of $C$ and the set of sets. Let $R$ be a presieve of $X$. Then a first object of $P$ and $R$ is an element of the function that maps $P$ and $R$ to the product object of the function that maps $f$ to the action of $P$ and $\operatorname{op}$ applied to $\operatorname{fst}$ applied to $f$.
Formula
$P \in \operatorname{Functor} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C), \mathrm{Set}), R \in \operatorname{Presieve} (X)\vdash \operatorname{FirstObj} (P , R):= \lambda P , R . \operatorname{piObj} (\lambda f . \operatorname{obj} (P , \operatorname{op} (\operatorname{fst} (f))))$
CategoryTheory.Equalizer.Presieve.Arrows.FirstObjdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.EqualizerSheafCondition, type
Wording
first object of P and X (from the name)
English
Let $P$ be a functor of the set of opposites of $C$ and the set of sets. Let $X$ be a function from elements of $I$ to elements of $C$. Then a first object of $P$ and $X$ is an element of the function that maps $P$ and $X$ to the product object of the function that maps $i$ to the action of $P$ and $\operatorname{op}$ applied to $X$ applied to $i$.
Formula
$P \in \operatorname{Functor} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C), \mathrm{Set}), X \in I \rightarrow C \vdash \operatorname{FirstObj} (P , X):= \lambda P , X . \operatorname{piObj} (\lambda i . \operatorname{obj} (P , \operatorname{op} (X (i))))$
CategoryTheory.Equalizer.Presieve.Arrows.SecondObjdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.EqualizerSheafCondition, type
Wording
second object of X and x1 (from the name)
English
Let $P$ be a functor of the set of opposites of $C$ and the set of sets. Let $X$ be a function from elements of $I$ to elements of $C$. Let $x_{1}$ be a function from elements $i$ of $I$ to homomorphisms of $X$ applied to $i$ and $B$. Then a second object of $X$ and $i$ is an element of the function that maps $P$, $X$, $x_{1}$ and $x_{1}$ to the product object of the function that maps $\mathrm{ij}$ to the action of $P$ and $\operatorname{op}$ applied to the pullback of $i$ applied to $\ProdfstMACRoZ {\mathrm{ij}}$ and $i$ applied to $\ProdsndMACRoAA {\mathrm{ij}}$.
Formula
$P \in \operatorname{Functor} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C), \mathrm{Set}), X \in I \rightarrow C , x_{1} \in I \rightarrow \operatorname{Hom} (X (i), B)\vdash \operatorname{SecondObj} (P , X , i , x_{1}):= \lambda P , X , x_{1} , x_{1} . \operatorname{piObj} (\lambda \mathrm{ij} . \operatorname{obj} (P , \operatorname{op} (\operatorname{pullback} (i (\ProdfstMACRoAG {\mathrm{ij}}), i (\ProdsndMACRoAH {\mathrm{ij}})))), x_{1})$

Wording
the first map of X and x1 (from the name)
English
Let $P$ be a functor of the set of opposites of $C$ and the set of sets. Let $X$ be a function from elements of $I$ to elements of $C$. Let $x_{1}$ be a function from elements $i$ of $I$ to homomorphisms of $X$ applied to $i$ and $B$. Wording

English

Let $x_{1}$ be a function from elements $i$ of $I$ to homomorphisms of $X$ applied to $i$ and $B$. CategoryTheory.Equivalence.sheafCongrdefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Equivalence, function or constant
Wording
the sheaf congruence of K and e (from the name)
English
Let $J$ be a Grothendieck topology of $C$. Let $K$ be a Grothendieck topology of $D$. Let $e$ be an equivalence of $C$ and $D$. Let $A$ be a set. Then the sheaf congruence of $K$ and $e$ is an equivalence of the set of sheaves of $J$ and $A$ and the set of sheaves of $K$ and $A$.
Formula
$J \in \operatorname{GrothendieckTopology} (C), K \in \operatorname{GrothendieckTopology} (D), e \in \operatorname{Equivalence} (C , D), A \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{sheafCongr} (J , K , e , A)\in \operatorname{Equivalence} (\operatorname{Sheaf} (J , A), \operatorname{Sheaf} (K , A))$
CategoryTheory.Equivalence.sheafCongr.counitIsodefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Equivalence, function or constant
Wording
the counit isomorphism of K and e (from the name)
English
Let $J$ be a Grothendieck topology of $C$. Let $K$ be a Grothendieck topology of $D$. Let $e$ be an equivalence of $C$ and $D$. Let $A$ be a set. Then the counit isomorphism of $K$ and $e$ is an isomorphism of the composition of the inverse of $K$ and $e$ and the functor of $K$ and $e$ and the identity of the set of sheaves of $K$ and $A$.
Formula
$J \in \operatorname{GrothendieckTopology} (C), K \in \operatorname{GrothendieckTopology} (D), e \in \operatorname{Equivalence} (C , D), A \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{counitIso} (J , K , e , A)\in \operatorname{Iso} (\operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{inverse} (J , K , e , A), \operatorname{functor} (J , K , e , A)), \operatorname{id} (\operatorname{Sheaf} (K , A)))$

CategoryTheory.Equivalence.transportAndSheafifydefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Equivalence, function or constant
Wording
the transport and sheafification of K and e (from the name)
English
Let $J$ be a Grothendieck topology of $C$. Let $K$ be a Grothendieck topology of $D$. Let $e$ be an equivalence of $C$ and $D$. Let $A$ be a set. Then the transport and sheafification of $K$ and $e$ is a functor of the set of functors of the set of opposites of $C$ and $A$ and the set of sheaves of $J$ and $A$ defined as the composition of the functor of the left congruence of the opposite of $e$ and the composition of the presheaf to sheaf of $K$ and $A$ and the inverse of the sheaf congruence of $K$ and $e$.
Formula
$J \in \operatorname{GrothendieckTopology} (C), K \in \operatorname{GrothendieckTopology} (D), e \in \operatorname{Equivalence} (C , D), A \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{transportAndSheafify} (J , K , e , A):= \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{functor} (\operatorname{congrLeft} (\operatorname{op} (e))), \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{presheafToSheaf} (K , A), \operatorname{inverse} (\operatorname{sheafCongr} (J , K , e , A))))\in \operatorname{Functor} (\operatorname{Functor} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C), A), \operatorname{Sheaf} (J , A))$

Then the transport sheafification adjunction of $K$ and $e$ is an adjunction of the transport and sheafification of $K$ and $e$ and the sheaf to presheaf of $J$ and $A$ defined as the adjunction of the composition of the composition of the adjunction of the left congruence of the opposite of $e$ and the sheafification adjunction of $K$ and $A$ and the adjunction of the inverse of the sheaf congruence of $K$ and $e$ and $\operatorname{transportIsoSheafToPresheaf}$ applied to $J$, $K$, $e$ and $A$.

Wording
the small sheafification of J and A (from the name)
English
Let $J$ be a Grothendieck topology of $C$. Let $A$ be a set. Then the small sheafification of $J$ and $A$ is a functor of the set of functors of the set of opposites of $C$ and $A$ and the set of sheaves of $J$ and $A$ defined as the transport and sheafification of the induced topology of the inverse of the equivalence small model of $C$ and $J$ and the equivalence small model of $C$.

CategoryTheory.GrothendieckTopology.RightOreConditiondefMathlib.CategoryTheory.Sites.Grothendieck, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $C$ be a set. Then $\operatorname{RightOreCondition}$ holds for $C$, if for all elements $X$, $Y$ and $Z$ of $C$, for all homomorphisms $\mathrm{yx}$ of $Y$ and $X$, for all homomorphisms $\mathrm{zx}$ of $Z$ and $X$, there exists an element $W$ of $C$, such that there exists a homomorphism $\mathrm{wy}$ of $W$ and $Y$, such that there exists a homomorphism $\mathrm{wz}$ of $W$ and $Z$, such that the composition of $\mathrm{wy}$ and $\mathrm{yx}$ is equal to the composition of $\mathrm{wz}$ and $\mathrm{zx}$.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{RightOreCondition} (C):\Leftrightarrow \forall X \in C . \forall Y \in C . \forall Z \in C . \forall \mathrm{yx} \in \operatorname{Hom} (Y , X). \forall \mathrm{zx} \in \operatorname{Hom} (Z , X). \exists W \in C . \exists \mathrm{wy} \in \operatorname{Hom} (W , Y). \exists \mathrm{wz} \in \operatorname{Hom} (W , Z). \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{comp} (\mathrm{wy} , \mathrm{yx})}{\operatorname{comp} (\mathrm{wz} , \mathrm{zx})}$

$J \in \operatorname{GrothendieckTopology} (C), D \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{toPlusNatTrans} (J , D)\in \operatorname{Hom} (\operatorname{id} (\operatorname{Functor} (\mathrm{Opposite} (C), D)), \operatorname{plusFunctor} (J , D))$
